## Summary: Existing Plan Cluster System and Reconciliation

I've thoroughly analyzed the codebase and here's a comprehensive breakdown of the living plan cluster system:

### 1. **Cluster Data Model** (`schema.py`)

```python
class Cluster(TypedDict, total=False):
    name: Required[str]           # Unique cluster identifier
    description: str | None       # Optional human-readable description
    finding_ids: list[str]        # Member finding IDs
    created_at: str               # ISO timestamp
    updated_at: str               # ISO timestamp
```

**Key characteristics:**
- Clusters are stored in `PlanModel["clusters"]` as a dict keyed by cluster name
- Each cluster tracks an ordered list of finding member IDs
- Cluster membership is also tracked via `ItemOverride["cluster"]` field for quick lookup

### 2. **Plan Data Model Structure** (`schema.py`)

The `PlanModel` TypedDict contains:
```python
class PlanModel(TypedDict, total=False):
    version: Required[int]
    created: Required[str]
    updated: Required[str]
    queue_order: list[str]        # User-ordered work queue
    deferred: list[str]           # (deprecated, migrated to skipped)
    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] # Skipped findings (temp/permanent/false_positive)
    active_cluster: str | None    # Currently focused cluster
    overrides: dict[str, ItemOverride]  # Per-finding metadata (description, note, cluster ref)
    clusters: dict[str, Cluster]  # All defined clusters
    superseded: dict[str, SupersededEntry]  # Findings that disappeared
```

### 3. **Cluster Operations** (`operations.py`)

**Create/Delete:**
- `create_cluster(plan, name, description)` — Creates new cluster, raises if exists
- `delete_cluster(plan, name)` — Deletes cluster, orphans members, clears override refs, clears active_cluster if focused

**Membership:**
- `add_to_cluster(plan, cluster_name, finding_ids)` — Adds IDs to cluster, updates override["cluster"] for each
- `remove_from_cluster(plan, cluster_name, finding_ids)` — Removes IDs, clears override["cluster"]

**Movement:**
- `move_cluster(plan, cluster_name, position, target, offset)` — Moves all cluster members as a contiguous block in queue_order
- Position types: "top", "bottom", "before", "after", "up N", "down N"

**Focus:**
- `set_focus(plan, cluster_name)` — Sets active_cluster focus
- `clear_focus(plan)` — Clears active_cluster

### 4. **How Overrides Track Cluster Membership**

```python
class ItemOverride(TypedDict, total=False):
    finding_id: Required[str]
    description: str | None
    note: str | None
    cluster: str | None          # <-- References cluster name
    created_at: str
    updated_at: str
```

**Two-way consistency:**
- When adding to cluster: creates/updates override with `cluster: cluster_name`
- When removing from cluster: clears override's cluster field to `None`
- Override is lazy-created if it doesn't exist

### 5. **Plan Ordering with Cluster Filtering** (`_work_queue/core.py`)

The `_apply_plan_order()` function:

1. **Annotation phase:**
   - Annotates each item with plan metadata (description, note, cluster info)
   - Builds `plan_cluster` dict containing: name, description, total_items, sibling_ids

2. **Ordering phase:**
   - Items in `queue_order` appear first (in that order)
   - Remaining items keep their mechanical sort
   - Skipped items appended last (optionally included)

3. **Cluster filtering:**
   ```python
   effective_cluster = cluster or active_cluster  # parameter or plan focus
   if effective_cluster:
       cluster_data = clusters.get(effective_cluster)
       cluster_member_ids = set(cluster_data.get("finding_ids", []))
       result = [item for item in result if item["id"] in cluster_member_ids]
   ```

**Key:** When a cluster is active, the entire result is filtered to only members of that cluster.

### 6. **Reconciliation Process** (`reconcile.py`)

Runs after scan (best-effort):

1. **Collects all referenced IDs** from:
   - `queue_order`
   - `skipped` keys
   - `overrides` keys
   - All cluster `finding_ids`

2. **Excludes already-superseded and synthetic IDs** (those starting with "subjective::")

3. **For each referenced ID:**
   - If it's no longer open/alive in state, moves it to `superseded`
   - Removes from queue_order, skipped, and all cluster finding_ids

4. **Resurfaces stale skips:**
   - Checks if any temporary skips have passed their `review_after` scan threshold
   - Moves them back to queue_order

5. **Prunes old superseded entries:**
   - Removes superseded entries older than 90 days
   - Cleans up their overrides

**What's NOT cleaned up:** Overrides are intentionally kept for history (descriptions/notes).

### 7. **Cluster Rendering** (`planning/render.py`)

**`_plan_user_ordered_section()`** renders clusters in the markdown plan:

1. Groups ordered items by cluster
2. For each cluster with members in queue_order:
   - Shows cluster name and description (blockquote)
   - Lists member findings with their overrides
3. Shows unclustered ordered items separately (if any clusters exist)

**Output example:**
```markdown
## User-Ordered Queue (25 items)

### Cluster: database-refactoring
> Refactoring database layer interactions

- [ ] [T1/high] Connection pool optimization
      `finder-12ab`
...

### (unclustered ordered items)

- [ ] [T2/medium] Cache invalidation bug
      `finder-34cd`
```

### 8. **Stale Dimension Sync** (`stale_dimensions.py`)

Separate from clusters but affects queue:

1. **Cleanup:** Removes `subjective::*` IDs from queue if dimension is no longer stale
2. **Injection:** If queue becomes empty after cleanup, injects all currently-stale dimension IDs

This allows subjective dimensions (user-assessed) to surface in queue when there are no mechanical findings.

### 9. **Grouping by Cluster** (`_work_queue/ranking.py`)

The `group_queue_items(items, group="cluster")` function:

```python
elif group == "cluster":
    plan_cluster = item.get("plan_cluster")
    key = plan_cluster["name"] if isinstance(plan_cluster, dict) else "(unclustered)"
```

Returns `{cluster_name: [items], "(unclustered)": [items]}` for rendering.

### 10. **No Existing Auto-Clustering Logic**

**Key finding:** There is **NO auto-grouping or auto-clustering logic** in the codebase. All clustering is:
- **Manual:** Users explicitly create clusters and add findings via CLI
- **Persistent:** Clusters are stored in plan.json and survive scans
- **Stateless w.r.t. findings:** Cluster membership doesn't change unless explicitly commanded

### 11. **CLI Cluster Commands** (`cluster_handlers.py`)

```
plan cluster create <name> [--description "..."]
plan cluster add <cluster> <patterns...>
plan cluster remove <cluster> <patterns...>
plan cluster delete <cluster>
plan cluster move <cluster> [top|bottom|before|after|up|down] [target/offset]
plan cluster list
```

All handlers use `resolve_ids_from_patterns()` to convert user patterns to finding IDs.

### 12. **Cascade Effects of Cluster Operations**

**Deletion:**
- Orphans all members
- Clears cluster refs from all overrides
- If it was active, clears active_cluster

**Reconciliation (scan):**
- When a finding disappears, it's removed from all cluster finding_ids
- Superseded entry is created with candidates for remapping
- No cascade to delete the cluster itself

**Purge (when findings removed):**
- Removes IDs from queue_order, skipped, and all cluster finding_ids
- Does NOT remove empty clusters

---

## Key Design Patterns

1. **Lazy override creation:** Cluster refs only created when needed
2. **Two-way consistency:** cluster finding_ids + override cluster field kept in sync
3. **Active focus:** Single active_cluster controls `next` and `status` views
4. **Best-effort reconciliation:** Plan changes are atomic, but reconciliation errors are swallowed
5. **No auto-clustering:** All clustering is explicit user action

This architecture gives you a solid foundation for **designing auto-clustering** — you can hook into the operations API and render the generated clusters without breaking existing manual clustering or reconciliation.

Excellent! Now I have all the information needed. Let me compile a comprehensive report:

## Complete Finding Data Model & Resolution Architecture

Based on my thorough reading of the codebase, here is everything you need to know for designing auto-clustering with batch resolution:

### 1. FINDING DATA STRUCTURE (TypedDict)

**Core Finding Dict** (`desloppify/engine/_state/schema.py`):

```python
Finding = {
    "id": str,                          # Stable ID: "detector::file::subtype" or "detector::file"
    "detector": str,                    # e.g., "unused", "coupling", "dict_keys", "review", etc.
    "file": str,                        # Relative path (normalized by rel())
    "tier": int,                        # 1-4 (severity level)
    "confidence": str,                  # e.g., "high", "medium", "low"
    "summary": str,                     # Human-readable title
    "detail": dict[str, Any],           # Detector-specific metadata
    "status": FindingStatus,            # "open" | "fixed" | "auto_resolved" | "wontfix" | "false_positive"
    "note": str | None,                 # Manual note (e.g., for reopens)
    "first_seen": str,                  # UTC timestamp when first detected
    "last_seen": str,                   # UTC timestamp of last scan seeing it
    "resolved_at": str | None,          # UTC when manually/auto resolved
    "reopen_count": int,                # Tracks how many times it reopened
    "suppressed": bool,                 # Optional, default False
    "suppressed_at": str | None,        # When suppression happened
    "suppression_pattern": str | None,  # Which ignore pattern matched
    "resolution_attestation": dict,     # Optional metadata about resolution
    "lang": str,                        # Optional language tag
    "zone": str,                        # Optional file zone (production/test/config/etc.)
}

FindingStatus = Literal[
    "open",
    "fixed",
    "auto_resolved",
    "wontfix",
    "false_positive"
]
```

### 2. FINDING ID ENCODING

**ID Format Pattern** (from `make_finding()` in `filtering.py`):
- **With subtype**: `"{detector}::{file}::{name}"`
- **Without subtype**: `"{detector}::{file}"`

Examples:
- `unused::src/utils.py::import_foo`
- `coupling::src/auth/index.ts`
- `dict_keys::src/config.py`

**Key properties:**
- Stable across rescans (same detector + file + subtype = same ID)
- IDs are URL-friendly (no special chars that break patterns)
- File paths are normalized relative to PROJECT_ROOT

### 3. PATTERN MATCHING (`match_findings()` in `resolution.py`)

**How patterns match finding IDs** (from `_matches_pattern()` in `filtering.py`):

```python
def _matches_pattern(finding_id: str, finding: dict, pattern: str) -> bool:
    return (
        finding_id == pattern                          # Exact ID match
        or ("*" in pattern and fnmatch.fnmatch(finding_id, pattern))  # Glob pattern
        or ("::" in pattern and finding_id.startswith(pattern))       # ID prefix match
        or finding.get("detector") == pattern                         # Detector name
        or finding["file"] == pattern                                 # Exact file path
        or finding["file"].startswith(pattern.rstrip("/") + "/")      # File directory
    )
```

**Pattern Examples:**
- `"dict_keys::*"` — all dict_keys findings (glob)
- `"unused::"` — prefix match (all unused findings)
- `"coupling"` — by detector name
- `"src/services/"` — by directory
- `"dict_keys::src/config.py"` — specific file + detector
- `"dict_keys::src/config.py::missing_key"` — exact ID

**Status filter:**
- `match_findings(state, pattern, status_filter="open")` — default, open only
- `status_filter="all"` — match any status

### 4. RESOLUTION PROCESS (`resolve_findings()`)

**Function signature:**
```python
def resolve_findings(
    state: StateModel,
    pattern: str,
    status: str,                    # Target status to set
    note: str | None = None,        # Manual note
    attestation: str | None = None, # Resolution justification
) -> list[str]:                     # Returns IDs that were resolved
```

**Resolution statuses:**
- `"fixed"` — manually marked as resolved
- `"wontfix"` — intentionally unresolved (captures scan_count snapshot)
- `"false_positive"` — incorrect detection
- `"open"` — reopen a previously resolved finding

**What resolution does:**
1. Finds all findings matching the pattern with appropriate status
2. For each matched finding:
   - Sets `status` to target
   - Sets `resolved_at` to current UTC timestamp
   - Clears suppression fields (reverts ignore suppression)
   - For `wontfix`: captures `wontfix_snapshot` with tier/confidence/detail at that scan count
   - For `open` (reopen): increments `reopen_count`, clears old attestation
   - Sets `resolution_attestation` dict:
     ```python
     {
         "kind": "manual" | "manual_reopen" | "scan_verified",
         "text": attestation or note,
         "attested_at": now,
         "scan_verified": False,  # True only for auto_resolved from scan
     }
     ```
3. Recomputes stats and validates invariants
4. Returns list of affected finding IDs

**Batch resolution via multiple patterns:**
```python
# From apply.py: _resolve_all_patterns()
all_resolved = []
for pattern in args.patterns:
    resolved = state_mod.resolve_findings(state, pattern, args.status, ...)
    all_resolved.extend(resolved)
```

### 5. WHAT HAPPENS ON RESCAN (`merge_scan()` in `merge.py`)

**Auto-resolution of disappeared findings** (`auto_resolve_disappeared()` in `merge_findings.py`):

When a finding doesn't appear in fresh scan output:
1. **Checks if finding should be auto-resolved:**
   - Must be in status: `"open"`, `"wontfix"`, `"fixed"`, or `"false_positive"` (not `"auto_resolved"`)
   - If finding's lang ≠ scan lang → **skip** (carry forward for next lang scan)
   - If finding's file is out of scope → **skip** (carry forward)
   - If finding's detector is "suspect" → **skip** (detector likely didn't run)
   - Otherwise → **auto-resolve**

2. **"Suspect detectors"** (`find_suspect_detectors()`) — detectors that likely didn't run:
   - Always suspects: `"review"` (import-only, never runs in scan)
   - Suspect if: had ≥3 open findings last scan, now has 0 findings (detector off?)
   - Suspect if: not in `ran_detectors` set (when potentials metadata provided)

3. **Auto-resolve sets:**
   ```python
   finding["status"] = "auto_resolved"
   finding["resolved_at"] = now
   finding["note"] = "Fixed despite wontfix — disappeared from scan (was wontfix)"
                   or "Disappeared from scan — likely fixed"
   finding["resolution_attestation"] = {
       "kind": "scan_verified",
       "text": "Disappeared from detector output",
       "attested_at": now,
       "scan_verified": True,  # <-- SCAN VERIFIED!
   }
   ```

**Key point:** When a finding disappears on rescan, it's **auto-resolved** (not just marked "fixed" — it gets special `scan_verified` attestation).

### 6. DETECTOR REGISTRY (`core/registry.py`)

**Complete detector list** with properties:

| Detector | Dimension | Action Type | Needs Judgment | Tier Notes |
|----------|-----------|------------|---|---|
| `logs` | Code quality | auto_fix | No | Quick wins |
| `unused` | Code quality | auto_fix | No | Fixers: unused-imports, unused-vars, unused-params |
| `exports` | Code quality | manual_fix | No | knip --fix needed |
| `deprecated` | Code quality | manual_fix | No | Remove/migrate |
| `structural` | File health | refactor | Yes | Large files |
| `props` | Code quality | refactor | Yes | Bloated components |
| `smells` | Code quality | auto_fix | Yes | Fixers: dead-useeffect, empty-if-chain |
| `react` | Code quality | refactor | Yes | Antipatterns |
| `coupling` | Code quality | reorganize | Yes | Tool: move |
| `cycles` | Security | reorganize | Yes | Tool: move |
| `orphaned` | Code quality | reorganize | Yes | Tool: move |
| `uncalled_functions` | Code quality | refactor | Yes | Dead code |
| `dict_keys` | Code quality | refactor | Yes | Schema mismatches |
| `dupes` | Duplication | refactor | Yes | Extract/consolidate |
| `test_coverage` | Test health | refactor | No | Add tests |
| `review` | Test health | refactor | Yes | From LLM import (never in scan) |
| `subjective_review` | Test health | manual_fix | Yes | Condition-based |
| `concerns` | Design coherence | refactor | No | From design review |
| `security` | Security | manual_fix | No | Prioritize by severity |

**Detection patterns:**
- `JUDGMENT_DETECTORS` = detectors needing human design judgment
- `fixers` = auto-fix tool IDs (cascade import cleanup, etc.)
- `tool` = special tool needed ("move", etc.)

### 7. CLUSTER DATA STRUCTURE (for your design)

**Cluster schema** (`engine/_plan/schema.py`):

```python
Cluster = {
    "name": str,                    # Cluster identifier
    "description": str | None,      # Human-readable purpose
    "finding_ids": list[str],       # List of finding IDs
    "created_at": str,              # UTC timestamp
    "updated_at": str,              # Last modified
}

# Stored in PlanModel["clusters"][name] = Cluster
```

**Cluster operations** (all in `engine/_plan/operations.py`):
- `create_cluster(plan, name, description)` — Create named cluster
- `add_to_cluster(plan, cluster_name, finding_ids)` — Add IDs
- `remove_from_cluster(plan, cluster_name, finding_ids)` — Remove IDs
- `delete_cluster(plan, name)` → list[str]` — Delete & return orphaned IDs
- `move_cluster(plan, cluster_name, position, ...)` — Move all members as block
- `set_focus(plan, cluster_name)` — Activate for `next` display
- `clear_focus(plan)` — Deactivate

**Cluster integration with overrides:**
```python
# When adding to cluster, also updates overrides:
overrides[finding_id]["cluster"] = cluster_name
overrides[finding_id]["updated_at"] = now
```

### 8. AUTO-CLUSTERING DESIGN IMPLICATIONS

**For batch cluster resolution:**

1. **Cluster resolution is plan-level, not state-level:**
   - Clusters are in `plan.json`, not in `state.json`
   - Resolving a cluster = `resolve_findings()` on all member IDs
   - You can resolve as: "fixed", "wontfix", "false_positive"

2. **Pattern options:**
   - Resolve by cluster name (build pattern from cluster members)
   - Pattern: `"detector::*"` resolves all of that detector
   - Pattern: `"detector::file::*"` resolves all subtypes in a file

3. **Workflow:**
   ```python
   # Pseudo-code for auto-cluster resolution
   cluster = plan["clusters"][cluster_name]
   member_ids = cluster["finding_ids"]
   
   # Create pattern matching all members
   patterns = [f"detector::{file}::*" for (detector, file) in member_ids]
   
   for pattern in patterns:
       resolved = state_mod.resolve_findings(
           state,
           pattern,
           status="fixed",  # or "wontfix", "false_positive"
           note=cluster.get("description"),
           attestation=f"Batch resolved via cluster {cluster_name}"
       )
   ```

4. **Re-scan safety:**
   - If a finding in cluster disappears → auto-resolved with `scan_verified=True`
   - If a finding reopens → `reopen_count` incremented, cluster membership unaffected
   - Clusters survive rescan (plan.json persists independently)

5. **Edge cases to handle:**
   - **Suppressed findings:** `match_findings()` already filters out suppressed
   - **Out-of-scope findings:** `resolve_findings()` doesn't skip them; only auto-resolve does during merge
   - **Detector changed:** Finding ID stays the same (detector name part is immutable)
   - **File renamed:** Creates new finding ID (file is part of ID); old one auto-resolved as "disappeared"

### 9. KEY PATTERNS FOR YOUR IMPLEMENTATION

**Immutable ID components:**
- ID encodes detection origin (detector + file + subtype)
- Changing detector name = breaking all associated finding IDs
- Changing file path = creation of new finding (old auto-resolves)

**Resolution is idempotent:**
- Resolving already-resolved finding = no-op (short-circuits if status matches)
- Re-scan auto-resolution bypasses manual resolution (uses `scan_verified` flag)

**Cluster members can be resolved independently:**
- Removing a finding from a cluster doesn't affect its resolution status
- Resolving a cluster member doesn't remove it from cluster
- Delete cluster → orphans remain in state (not deleted)

**Batch patterns are flexible:**
- Can resolve by: ID, glob, prefix, detector name, file path, directory
- Each pattern is matched independently (can overlap)
- Duplicates in resolution list are silently deduplicated (dict-based state)

---

This is the complete finding model. For auto-clustering, the key insight is:
- **Findings live in state** (mutable, scanned)
- **Clusters live in plan** (hand-curated, persistent)
- **Resolution bridges both** (updates finding status in state based on cluster decisions in plan)

> DEVELOPER

## Task

Design an auto-clustering system for desloppify's work queue so that related findings are grouped into "tasks" rather than shown as individual items.

## Context

Desloppify is a Python CLI tool for codebase health. It scans code, produces findings, and presents them via `next` (single item) and `plan` (overview). The problem: 819 individual findings are presented one-at-a-time. But they naturally cluster into ~50 tasks. For example:
- 6 unused imports → 1 task: "clean up unused references"
- 15 dict_keys phantom_read findings in one file → 1 task: "decide on phantom reads in mechanical.py"
- 4 dict_keys schema_drift findings → 1 task: "fix schema drift issues"
- 6 uncalled functions → 1 task: "remove dead functions"

## Current Architecture

### Finding IDs
Format: `detector::file::subtype::extra` or shorter variants.
Examples:
- `unused::src/utils.py::import_foo`
- `dict_keys::src/config.py::phantom_read::confidence::45`
- `smells::src/auth.py::monster_function`

### Work Queue (what `next` shows)
Built in `engine/_work_queue/core.py`:
1. `build_finding_items()` → item dicts from state findings
2. `build_subjective_items()` → synthetic T4 items
3. Sort by `item_sort_key()` → (tier, confidence, weight, count, id)
4. `_apply_plan_order()` → plan reorders + annotates
5. Tier filter + count limit

Each item dict has: id, kind, detector, file, tier, effective_tier, confidence, summary, detail, status, primary_command, plus plan overlay fields (queue_position, plan_description, plan_note, plan_cluster, plan_skipped).

### Existing Clusters (manual only)
In `engine/_plan/operations.py`:
- `create_cluster(plan, name, description)`
- `add_to_cluster(plan, cluster_name, finding_ids)`
- `remove_from_cluster(plan, cluster_name, finding_ids)`
- `delete_cluster(plan, name)`
- `move_cluster(plan, cluster_name, position, ...)`
- `set_focus(plan, cluster_name)` / `clear_focus(plan)`

Clusters stored in plan.json:
```json
"clusters": {
  "name": {
    "name": "my-cluster",
    "description": "...",
    "finding_ids": ["id1", "id2"],
    "created_at": "...",
    "updated_at": "..."
  }
}
```

### Rendering
`_render_item()` in `next_parts/render.py` renders one item at a time. Already shows plan_cluster info if present.

### Grouping (existing but underused)
`group_queue_items()` in `ranking.py` supports grouping by: item, file, detector, tier, cluster. But only used with `group="item"` by default.

### Pattern matching for resolution
`match_findings(state, pattern)` matches by: exact ID, glob, prefix, detector name, file path, directory.

## User's Requirements
1. Auto-cluster related findings into "tasks" 
2. `next` shows tasks (clusters) not individual findings
3. Each task has a clear resolution action (run command, fix manually, batch resolve)
4. User can de-cluster individual items (e.g., fixed 48 of 50, leave 2 undone)
5. Preserve all underlying finding data — clustering is a view layer, not data loss
6. Tiers still work as priority — a T1 cluster outranks a T2 cluster
7. Plan integration — auto-clusters should be real plan clusters that users can reorder, skip, etc.

## Design Constraints
- Auto-clustering runs during reconciliation (after scan), not during queue building (too late)
- Must not break existing manual clusters
- Must be deterministic (same findings → same clusters)
- Must handle rescans gracefully (findings appear/disappear)
- Must work with existing resolve patterns (batch resolve a cluster)

## Questions to Address
1. What's the clustering algorithm? (detector + file? detector + subtype? something smarter?)
2. Where does auto-clustering run? (reconciliation? separate command? both?)
3. How do auto-clusters differ from manual clusters? (naming convention? metadata flag?)
4. How does `next` render a cluster vs a single item?
5. How does resolution work for clusters?
6. What happens when a user removes an item from an auto-cluster?
7. What happens on rescan — do auto-clusters get regenerated?
8. How do auto-clusters interact with the tier system?

## Please Design
A concrete implementation plan with:
- Data model changes
- New functions and where they live
- Algorithm for clustering
- Rendering changes
- Resolution flow
- Rescan/reconciliation behavior
- Files to modify with specific changes
- Edge cases and their handling

```
1→"""Unified work-queue selection for next/show/plan views."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from dataclasses import dataclass
     6→from typing import TypedDict
     7→
     8→from desloppify.engine._work_queue.helpers import (
     9→    ALL_STATUSES,
    10→    ATTEST_EXAMPLE,
    11→    build_subjective_items,
    12→    scope_matches,
    13→)
    14→from desloppify.engine._work_queue.ranking import (
    15→    build_finding_items,
    16→    choose_fallback_tier,
    17→    group_queue_items,
    18→    item_explain,

    22→from desloppify.state import StateModel
    23→
    24→
    25→@dataclass(frozen=True)
    26→class QueueBuildOptions:
    27→    """Configuration for queue construction and tier selection behavior."""
    28→
    29→    tier: int | None = None
    30→    count: int | None = 1
    31→    scan_path: str | None = None
    32→    scope: str | None = None
    33→    status: str = "open"
    34→    include_subjective: bool = True
    35→    subjective_threshold: float = 100.0
    36→    chronic: bool = False
    37→    no_tier_fallback: bool = False
    38→    explain: bool = False
    39→    plan: dict | None = None
    40→    include_skipped: bool = False
    41→    cluster: str | None = None
    42→
    43→
    44→class WorkQueueResult(TypedDict):
    45→    """Typed shape of the dict returned by :func:`build_work_queue`."""
    46→
    47→    items: list[dict]
    48→    total: int
    49→    tier_counts: dict[int, int]
    50→    requested_tier: int | None
    51→    selected_tier: int | None
    52→    fallback_reason: str | None
    53→    available_tiers: list[int]
    54→    grouped: dict[str, list[dict]]
    55→
    56→
    57→def _apply_plan_order(
    58→    items: list[dict],
    59→    plan: dict,
    60→    *,
    61→    include_skipped: bool = False,
    62→    cluster: str | None = None,
    63→) -> list[dict]:
    64→    """Reorder items according to the living plan.
    65→
    66→    1. Items in ``queue_order`` appear first, in that order.
    67→    2. Remaining items keep their mechanical sort.
    68→    3. Skipped items are appended last (or excluded).
    69→    4. Each item is annotated with plan metadata.
    70→    """
    71→    queue_order: list[str] = plan.get("queue_order", [])
    72→    skipped_map: dict = plan.get("skipped", {})
    73→    skipped_ids: set[str] = set(skipped_map.keys())
    74→    overrides: dict = plan.get("overrides", {})
    75→    clusters: dict = plan.get("clusters", {})
    76→    active_cluster = plan.get("active_cluster")
    77→
    78→    # Build lookup
    79→    by_id: dict[str, dict] = {}
    80→    for item in items:
    81→        by_id[item["id"]] = item
    82→
    83→    # Annotate items with plan metadata
    84→    for item_id, item in by_id.items():
    85→        override = overrides.get(item_id, {})
    86→        if override.get("description"):
    87→            item["plan_description"] = override["description"]
    88→        if override.get("note"):
    89→            item["plan_note"] = override["note"]
    90→        if override.get("cluster"):
    91→            cluster_name = override["cluster"]
    92→            cluster_data = clusters.get(cluster_name, {})
    93→            item["plan_cluster"] = {
    94→                "name": cluster_name,
    95→                "description": cluster_data.get("description"),
    96→                "total_items": len(cluster_data.get("finding_ids", [])),
    97→                "sibling_ids": cluster_data.get("finding_ids", []),
    98→            }
    99→
   100→    # Split into ordered, remaining, skipped
   101→    ordered: list[dict] = []
   102→    ordered_ids: set[str] = set()
   103→    for fid in queue_order:
   104→        if fid in by_id and fid not in skipped_ids:
   105→            ordered.append(by_id[fid])
   106→            ordered_ids.add(fid)
   107→
   108→    skipped_items: list[dict] = []
   109→    remaining: list[dict] = []
   110→    for item in items:
   111→        item_id = item["id"]
   112→        if item_id in ordered_ids:
   113→            continue
   114→        if item_id in skipped_ids:
   115→            skipped_items.append(item)
   116→        else:
   117→            remaining.append(item)
   118→
   119→    # Assign queue positions
   120→    result = ordered + remaining
   121→    if include_skipped:
   122→        result = result + skipped_items
   123→
   124→    for pos, item in enumerate(result):
   125→        item["queue_position"] = pos + 1
   126→        if item["id"] in skipped_ids:
   127→            item["plan_skipped"] = True
   128→            skip_entry = skipped_map.get(item["id"])
   129→            if skip_entry:
   130→                item["plan_skip_kind"] = skip_entry.get("kind", "temporary")
   131→                skip_reason = skip_entry.get("reason")
   132→                if skip_reason:
   133→                    item["plan_skip_reason"] = skip_reason
   134→
   135→    # Filter to cluster if requested
   136→    effective_cluster = cluster or active_cluster
   137→    if effective_cluster:
   138→        cluster_data = clusters.get(effective_cluster, {})
   139→        cluster_member_ids = set(cluster_data.get("finding_ids", []))
   140→        if cluster_member_ids:
   141→            result = [item for item in result if item["id"] in cluster_member_ids]
   142→
   143→    return result
   144→
   145→
   146→def build_work_queue(
   147→    state: StateModel,
   148→    *,
   149→    options: QueueBuildOptions | None = None,
   150→) -> WorkQueueResult:
   151→    """Build ranked queue items + tier metadata."""
   152→    resolved_options = options or QueueBuildOptions()
   153→
   154→    status = resolved_options.status
   155→    if status not in ALL_STATUSES:
   156→        raise ValueError(f"Unsupported status filter: {status}")
   157→    try:
   158→        subjective_threshold_value = float(resolved_options.subjective_threshold)
   159→    except (TypeError, ValueError):
   160→        subjective_threshold_value = 100.0
   161→    subjective_threshold_value = max(0.0, min(100.0, subjective_threshold_value))
   162→
   163→    finding_items = build_finding_items(
   164→        state,
   165→        scan_path=resolved_options.scan_path,
   166→        status_filter=status,
   167→        scope=resolved_options.scope,
   168→        chronic=resolved_options.chronic,
   169→    )
   170→
   171→    all_items = list(finding_items)
   172→    if (
   173→        resolved_options.include_subjective
   174→        and status in {"open", "all"}
   175→        and not resolved_options.chronic
   176→    ):
   177→        subjective_items = build_subjective_items(
   178→            state,
   179→            state.get("findings", {}),
   180→            threshold=subjective_threshold_value,
   181→        )
   182→        for item in subjective_items:
   183→            if scope_matches(item, resolved_options.scope):
   184→                all_items.append(item)
   185→
   186→    all_items.sort(key=item_sort_key)
   187→
   188→    # Apply living plan ordering if provided
   189→    if resolved_options.plan:
   190→        all_items = _apply_plan_order(
   191→            all_items,
   192→            resolved_options.plan,
   193→            include_skipped=resolved_options.include_skipped,
   194→            cluster=resolved_options.cluster,
   195→        )
   196→
   197→    counts = tier_counts(all_items)
   198→
   199→    requested_tier = (
   200→        int(resolved_options.tier) if resolved_options.tier is not None else None
   201→    )
   202→    selected_tier = requested_tier
   203→    fallback_reason = None
   204→    filtered = all_items
   205→
   206→    if requested_tier is not None:
   207→        filtered = [
   208→            item
   209→            for item in all_items
   210→            if int(item.get("effective_tier", item.get("tier", 3))) == requested_tier
   211→        ]
   212→        if not filtered and not resolved_options.no_tier_fallback:
   213→            chosen = choose_fallback_tier(requested_tier, counts)
   214→            if chosen is not None:
   215→                selected_tier = chosen
   216→                filtered = [
   217→                    item
   218→                    for item in all_items
   219→                    if int(item.get("effective_tier", item.get("tier", 3))) == chosen
   220→                ]
   221→                fallback_reason = (
   222→                    f"Requested T{requested_tier} has 0 open -> showing T{chosen} "
   223→                    "(nearest non-empty)."
   224→                )
   225→        elif not filtered:
   226→            fallback_reason = f"Requested T{requested_tier} has 0 open."
   227→
   228→    total = len(filtered)
   229→    if resolved_options.count is not None and resolved_options.count > 0:
   230→        filtered = filtered[: resolved_options.count]
   231→
   232→    if resolved_options.explain:
   233→        for item in filtered:
   234→            item["explain"] = item_explain(item)
   235→
   236→    available_tiers = [tier for tier, value in counts.items() if value > 0]
   237→    return {
   238→        "items": filtered,
   239→        "total": total,
   240→        "tier_counts": counts,
   241→        "requested_tier": requested_tier,
   242→        "selected_tier": selected_tier,
   243→        "fallback_reason": fallback_reason,
   244→        "available_tiers": available_tiers,
   245→        "grouped": group_queue_items(filtered, "item"),
   246→    }
   247→
   248→
   249→__all__ = [
   250→    "ATTEST_EXAMPLE",
   251→    "QueueBuildOptions",
   252→    "WorkQueueResult",
   253→    "build_work_queue",
   254→    "group_queue_items",
   255→]
   256→
```

```
1→"""Pure mutation operations on the plan model."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify.engine._plan.schema import (
     6→    Cluster,
     7→    PlanModel,
     8→    SkipEntry,
     9→    ensure_plan_defaults,
    10→)
    11→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import utc_now
    12→
    13→
    14→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    15→# Helpers
    16→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    17→
    18→def _remove_id_from_lists(plan: PlanModel, finding_id: str) -> None:
    19→    """Remove a finding ID from queue_order and skipped."""
    20→    order: list[str] = plan["queue_order"]
    21→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] = plan.get("skipped", {})
    22→    if finding_id in order:
    23→        order.remove(finding_id)
    24→    skipped.pop(finding_id, None)
    25→
    26→
    27→def _resolve_position(
    28→    order: list[str],
    29→    position: str,
    30→    target: str | None = None,
    31→    offset: int | None = None,
    32→    finding_ids: list[str] | None = None,
    33→) -> int:
    34→    """Resolve a position specifier to an insertion index.
    35→
    36→    *finding_ids* are the IDs being moved — used to calculate relative
    37→    positions when they already exist in the list.
    38→    """
    39→    moving = set(finding_ids or [])
    40→
    41→    if position == "top":
    42→        return 0
    43→    if position == "bottom":
    44→        return len(order)
    45→
    46→    if position == "before" and target:
    47→        for i, item_id in enumerate(order):
    48→            if item_id == target and item_id not in moving:
    49→                return i
    50→        return 0
    51→
    52→    if position == "after" and target:
    53→        for i, item_id in enumerate(order):
    54→            if item_id == target and item_id not in moving:
    55→                return i + 1
    56→        return len(order)
    57→
    58→    if position == "up" and offset is not None:
    59→        if not finding_ids:
    60→            return 0
    61→        # Find current position of first item
    62→        first_id = finding_ids[0]
    63→        current_idx = None
    64→        clean_order = [x for x in order if x not in moving]
    65→        for i, item_id in enumerate(clean_order):
    66→            if item_id == first_id:
    67→                current_idx = i
    68→                break
    69→        if current_idx is None:
    70→            # Item not yet in order; treat as "insert at position offset from top"
    71→            return max(0, len(clean_order) - offset)
    72→        return max(0, current_idx - offset)
    73→
    74→    if position == "down" and offset is not None:
    75→        if not finding_ids:
    76→            return len(order)
    77→        first_id = finding_ids[0]
    78→        clean_order = [x for x in order if x not in moving]
    79→        current_idx = None
    80→        for i, item_id in enumerate(clean_order):
    81→            if item_id == first_id:
    82→                current_idx = i
    83→                break
    84→        if current_idx is None:
    85→            return len(clean_order)
    86→        return min(len(clean_order), current_idx + offset)
    87→
    88→    return len(order)
    89→
    90→
    91→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    92→# Move items
    93→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    94→
    95→def move_items(
    96→    plan: PlanModel,
    97→    finding_ids: list[str],
    98→    position: str,
    99→    target: str | None = None,
   100→    offset: int | None = None,
   101→) -> int:
   102→    """Move finding IDs to a position in queue_order. Returns count moved."""
   103→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   104→    order: list[str] = plan["queue_order"]
   105→
   106→    # Remove from skipped if present
   107→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] = plan.get("skipped", {})
   108→    for fid in finding_ids:
   109→        skipped.pop(fid, None)
   110→
   111→    # Remove from current position in order
   112→    for fid in finding_ids:
   113→        if fid in order:
   114→            order.remove(fid)
   115→
   116→    # Resolve insertion point
   117→    idx = _resolve_position(order, position, target, offset, finding_ids)
   118→
   119→    # Insert in original order
   120→    for i, fid in enumerate(finding_ids):
   121→        order.insert(idx + i, fid)
   122→
   123→    return len(finding_ids)
   124→
   125→
   126→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   127→# Skip / unskip
   128→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   129→
   130→def skip_items(
   131→    plan: PlanModel,
   132→    finding_ids: list[str],
   133→    *,
   134→    kind: str = "temporary",
   135→    reason: str | None = None,
   136→    note: str | None = None,
   137→    attestation: str | None = None,
   138→    review_after: int | None = None,
   139→    scan_count: int = 0,
   140→) -> int:
   141→    """Move finding IDs to the skipped dict. Returns count skipped."""
   142→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   143→    now = utc_now()
   144→    count = 0
   145→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] = plan["skipped"]
   146→    for fid in finding_ids:
   147→        _remove_id_from_lists(plan, fid)
   148→        skipped[fid] = {
   149→            "finding_id": fid,
   150→            "kind": kind,
   151→            "reason": reason,
   152→            "note": note,
   153→            "attestation": attestation,
   154→            "created_at": now,
   155→            "review_after": review_after,
   156→            "skipped_at_scan": scan_count,
   157→        }
   158→        count += 1
   159→    return count
   160→
   161→
   162→def unskip_items(
   163→    plan: PlanModel, finding_ids: list[str]
   164→) -> tuple[int, list[str]]:
   165→    """Bring finding IDs back from skipped to the end of queue_order.
   166→
   167→    Returns ``(count_unskipped, permanent_ids_needing_state_reopen)``
   168→    where the second list contains IDs that were permanent or false_positive
   169→    and need their state-layer status reopened by the caller.
   170→    """
   171→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   172→    count = 0
   173→    need_reopen: list[str] = []
   174→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] = plan["skipped"]
   175→    for fid in finding_ids:
   176→        entry = skipped.pop(fid, None)
   177→        if entry is not None:
   178→            if entry.get("kind") in ("permanent", "false_positive"):
   179→                need_reopen.append(fid)
   180→            if fid not in plan["queue_order"]:
   181→                plan["queue_order"].append(fid)
   182→            count += 1
   183→    return count, need_reopen
   184→
   185→
   186→def resurface_stale_skips(
   187→    plan: PlanModel, current_scan_count: int
   188→) -> list[str]:
   189→    """Move temporary skips past their review_after threshold back to queue.
   190→
   191→    Returns list of resurfaced finding IDs.
   192→    """
   193→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   194→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] = plan["skipped"]
   195→    resurfaced: list[str] = []
   196→    for fid in list(skipped):
   197→        entry = skipped[fid]
   198→        if entry.get("kind") != "temporary":
   199→            continue
   200→        review_after = entry.get("review_after")
   201→        if review_after is None:
   202→            continue
   203→        skipped_at = entry.get("skipped_at_scan", 0)
   204→        if current_scan_count >= skipped_at + review_after:
   205→            skipped.pop(fid)
   206→            if fid not in plan["queue_order"]:
   207→                plan["queue_order"].append(fid)
   208→            resurfaced.append(fid)
   209→    return resurfaced
   210→
   211→
   212→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   213→# Describe / annotate
   214→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   215→
   216→def describe_finding(
   217→    plan: PlanModel, finding_id: str, description: str | None
   218→) -> None:
   219→    """Set or clear an augmented description on a finding."""
   220→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   221→    now = utc_now()
   222→    overrides = plan["overrides"]
   223→    if finding_id not in overrides:
   224→        overrides[finding_id] = {"finding_id": finding_id, "created_at": now}
   225→    overrides[finding_id]["description"] = description
   226→    overrides[finding_id]["updated_at"] = now
   227→
   228→
   229→def annotate_finding(
   230→    plan: PlanModel, finding_id: str, note: str | None
   231→) -> None:
   232→    """Set or clear a note on a finding."""
   233→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   234→    now = utc_now()
   235→    overrides = plan["overrides"]
   236→    if finding_id not in overrides:
   237→        overrides[finding_id] = {"finding_id": finding_id, "created_at": now}
   238→    overrides[finding_id]["note"] = note
   239→    overrides[finding_id]["updated_at"] = now
   240→
   241→
   242→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   243→# Clusters
   244→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   245→
   246→def create_cluster(
   247→    plan: PlanModel, name: str, description: str | None = None
   248→) -> Cluster:
   249→    """Create a named cluster. Raises ValueError if it already exists."""
   250→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   251→    if name in plan["clusters"]:
   252→        raise ValueError(f"Cluster {name!r} already exists")
   253→    now = utc_now()
   254→    cluster: Cluster = {
   255→        "name": name,
   256→        "description": description,
   257→        "finding_ids": [],
   258→        "created_at": now,
   259→        "updated_at": now,
   260→    }
   261→    plan["clusters"][name] = cluster
   262→    return cluster
   263→
   264→
   265→def add_to_cluster(
   266→    plan: PlanModel, cluster_name: str, finding_ids: list[str]
   267→) -> int:
   268→    """Add finding IDs to a cluster. Returns count added."""
   269→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   270→    cluster = plan["clusters"].get(cluster_name)
   271→    if cluster is None:
   272→        raise ValueError(f"Cluster {cluster_name!r} does not exist")
   273→
   274→    member_ids: list[str] = cluster["finding_ids"]
   275→    count = 0
   276→    now = utc_now()
   277→    for fid in finding_ids:
   278→        if fid not in member_ids:
   279→            member_ids.append(fid)
   280→            count += 1
   281→        # Update override to track cluster membership
   282→        overrides = plan["overrides"]
   283→        if fid not in overrides:
   284→            overrides[fid] = {"finding_id": fid, "created_at": now}
   285→        overrides[fid]["cluster"] = cluster_name
   286→        overrides[fid]["updated_at"] = now
   287→
   288→    cluster["updated_at"] = now
   289→    return count
   290→
   291→
   292→def remove_from_cluster(
   293→    plan: PlanModel, cluster_name: str, finding_ids: list[str]
   294→) -> int:
   295→    """Remove finding IDs from a cluster. Returns count removed."""
   296→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   297→    cluster = plan["clusters"].get(cluster_name)
   298→    if cluster is None:
   299→        raise ValueError(f"Cluster {cluster_name!r} does not exist")
   300→
   301→    member_ids: list[str] = cluster["finding_ids"]
   302→    now = utc_now()
   303→    count = 0
   304→    for fid in finding_ids:
   305→        if fid in member_ids:
   306→            member_ids.remove(fid)
   307→            count += 1
   308→        # Clear cluster from override
   309→        override = plan["overrides"].get(fid)
   310→        if override and override.get("cluster") == cluster_name:
   311→            override["cluster"] = None
   312→            override["updated_at"] = now
   313→
   314→    cluster["updated_at"] = now
   315→    return count
   316→
   317→
   318→def delete_cluster(plan: PlanModel, name: str) -> list[str]:
   319→    """Delete a cluster and clear cluster refs from overrides. Returns orphaned IDs."""
   320→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   321→    cluster = plan["clusters"].pop(name, None)
   322→    if cluster is None:
   323→        raise ValueError(f"Cluster {name!r} does not exist")
   324→
   325→    orphaned = list(cluster.get("finding_ids", []))
   326→    now = utc_now()
   327→    for fid in orphaned:
   328→        override = plan["overrides"].get(fid)
   329→        if override and override.get("cluster") == name:
   330→            override["cluster"] = None
   331→            override["updated_at"] = now
   332→
   333→    if plan.get("active_cluster") == name:
   334→        plan["active_cluster"] = None
   335→
   336→    return orphaned
   337→
   338→
   339→def move_cluster(
   340→    plan: PlanModel,
   341→    cluster_name: str,
   342→    position: str,
   343→    target: str | None = None,
   344→    offset: int | None = None,
   345→) -> int:
   346→    """Move all cluster members as a contiguous block. Returns count moved."""
   347→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   348→    cluster = plan["clusters"].get(cluster_name)
   349→    if cluster is None:
   350→        raise ValueError(f"Cluster {cluster_name!r} does not exist")
   351→
   352→    member_ids = list(cluster.get("finding_ids", []))
   353→    if not member_ids:
   354→        return 0
   355→
   356→    return move_items(plan, member_ids, position, target, offset)
   357→
   358→
   359→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   360→# Focus
   361→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   362→
   363→def set_focus(plan: PlanModel, cluster_name: str) -> None:
   364→    """Set the active cluster focus."""
   365→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   366→    if cluster_name not in plan["clusters"]:
   367→        raise ValueError(f"Cluster {cluster_name!r} does not exist")
   368→    plan["active_cluster"] = cluster_name
   369→
   370→
   371→def clear_focus(plan: PlanModel) -> None:
   372→    """Clear the active cluster focus."""
   373→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   374→    plan["active_cluster"] = None
   375→
   376→
   377→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   378→# Reset
   379→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   380→
   381→def reset_plan(plan: PlanModel) -> None:
   382→    """Reset plan to empty state, preserving version and created timestamp."""
   383→    created = plan.get("created", utc_now())
   384→    plan.clear()
   385→    from desloppify.engine._plan.schema import empty_plan as _empty
   386→    for k, v in _empty().items():
   387→        plan[k] = v
   388→    plan["created"] = created
   389→
   390→
   391→def purge_ids(plan: PlanModel, finding_ids: list[str]) -> int:
   392→    """Remove finding IDs from the plan entirely.
   393→
   394→    Cleans queue_order, skipped, and all cluster memberships.
   395→    Does NOT touch overrides (descriptions/notes are kept for history).
   396→    Returns count of IDs that were actually present somewhere.
   397→    """
   398→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   399→    found = 0
   400→
   401→    order: list[str] = plan["queue_order"]
   402→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] = plan["skipped"]
   403→    for fid in finding_ids:
   404→        was_present = False
   405→        if fid in order:
   406→            order.remove(fid)
   407→            was_present = True
   408→        if fid in skipped:
   409→            skipped.pop(fid)
   410→            was_present = True
   411→        for cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).values():
   412→            ids = cluster.get("finding_ids", [])
   413→            if fid in ids:
   414→                ids.remove(fid)
   415→                was_present = True
   416→        if was_present:
   417→            found += 1
   418→
   419→    return found
   420→
   421→
   422→__all__ = [
   423→    "add_to_cluster",
   424→    "annotate_finding",
   425→    "clear_focus",
   426→    "create_cluster",
   427→    "delete_cluster",
   428→    "describe_finding",
   429→    "move_cluster",
   430→    "move_items",
   431→    "purge_ids",
   432→    "remove_from_cluster",
   433→    "reset_plan",
   434→    "resurface_stale_skips",
   435→    "set_focus",
   436→    "skip_items",
   437→    "unskip_items",
   438→]
   439→
```

```
1→"""Plan schema types, defaults, and validation."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from typing import Any, Required, TypedDict
     6→
     7→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import utc_now
     8→
     9→PLAN_VERSION = 2
    10→
    11→VALID_SKIP_KINDS = {"temporary", "permanent", "false_positive"}
    12→
    13→
    14→class SkipEntry(TypedDict, total=False):
    15→    finding_id: Required[str]
    16→    kind: Required[str]  # "temporary" | "permanent" | "false_positive"
    17→    reason: str | None
    18→    note: str | None  # required for permanent (wontfix note)
    19→    attestation: str | None  # required for permanent/false_positive
    20→    created_at: str
    21→    review_after: int | None  # re-surface after N scans (temporary only)
    22→    skipped_at_scan: int  # state.scan_count when skipped
    23→
    24→
    25→class ItemOverride(TypedDict, total=False):
    26→    finding_id: Required[str]
    27→    description: str | None
    28→    note: str | None
    29→    cluster: str | None
    30→    created_at: str
    31→    updated_at: str
    32→
    33→
    34→class Cluster(TypedDict, total=False):
    35→    name: Required[str]
    36→    description: str | None
    37→    finding_ids: list[str]
    38→    created_at: str
    39→    updated_at: str
    40→
    41→
    42→class SupersededEntry(TypedDict, total=False):
    43→    original_id: Required[str]
    44→    original_detector: str
    45→    original_file: str
    46→    original_summary: str
    47→    status: str  # "superseded" | "remapped" | "dismissed"
    48→    superseded_at: str
    49→    remapped_to: str | None
    50→    candidates: list[str]
    51→    note: str | None
    52→
    53→
    54→class PlanModel(TypedDict, total=False):
    55→    version: Required[int]
    56→    created: Required[str]
    57→    updated: Required[str]
    58→    queue_order: list[str]
    59→    deferred: list[str]  # kept empty for migration compat
    60→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry]
    61→    active_cluster: str | None
    62→    overrides: dict[str, ItemOverride]
    63→    clusters: dict[str, Cluster]
    64→    superseded: dict[str, SupersededEntry]
    65→
    66→
    67→def empty_plan() -> PlanModel:
    68→    """Return a new empty plan payload."""
    69→    now = utc_now()
    70→    return {
    71→        "version": PLAN_VERSION,
    72→        "created": now,
    73→        "updated": now,
    74→        "queue_order": [],
    75→        "deferred": [],
    76→        "skipped": {},
    77→        "active_cluster": None,
    78→        "overrides": {},
    79→        "clusters": {},
    80→        "superseded": {},
    81→    }
    82→
    83→
    84→def ensure_plan_defaults(plan: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
    85→    """Normalize a loaded plan to ensure all keys exist.
    86→
    87→    Handles migration from v1 (deferred list) to v2 (skipped dict).
    88→    """
    89→    defaults = empty_plan()
    90→    for key, value in defaults.items():
    91→        plan.setdefault(key, value)
    92→
    93→    if not isinstance(plan.get("queue_order"), list):
    94→        plan["queue_order"] = []
    95→    if not isinstance(plan.get("deferred"), list):
    96→        plan["deferred"] = []
    97→    if not isinstance(plan.get("skipped"), dict):
    98→        plan["skipped"] = {}
    99→    if not isinstance(plan.get("overrides"), dict):
   100→        plan["overrides"] = {}
   101→    if not isinstance(plan.get("clusters"), dict):
   102→        plan["clusters"] = {}
   103→    if not isinstance(plan.get("superseded"), dict):
   104→        plan["superseded"] = {}
   105→
   106→    # Migrate deferred → skipped (v1 → v2)
   107→    deferred: list[str] = plan["deferred"]
   108→    skipped: dict[str, SkipEntry] = plan["skipped"]
   109→    if deferred:
   110→        now = utc_now()
   111→        for fid in list(deferred):
   112→            if fid not in skipped:
   113→                skipped[fid] = {
   114→                    "finding_id": fid,
   115→                    "kind": "temporary",
   116→                    "reason": None,
   117→                    "note": None,
   118→                    "attestation": None,
   119→                    "created_at": now,
   120→                    "review_after": None,
   121→                    "skipped_at_scan": 0,
   122→                }
   123→        deferred.clear()
   124→
   125→    # Normalize cluster finding_ids
   126→    for cluster in plan["clusters"].values():
   127→        if isinstance(cluster, dict):
   128→            if not isinstance(cluster.get("finding_ids"), list):
   129→                cluster["finding_ids"] = []
   130→
   131→
   132→def validate_plan(plan: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
   133→    """Raise ValueError when plan invariants are violated."""
   134→    if not isinstance(plan.get("version"), int):
   135→        raise ValueError("plan.version must be an int")
   136→    if not isinstance(plan.get("queue_order"), list):
   137→        raise ValueError("plan.queue_order must be a list")
   138→
   139→    # No ID should appear in both queue_order and skipped
   140→    skipped_ids = set(plan.get("skipped", {}).keys())
   141→    overlap = set(plan["queue_order"]) & skipped_ids
   142→    if overlap:
   143→        raise ValueError(
   144→            f"IDs cannot appear in both queue_order and skipped: {sorted(overlap)}"
   145→        )
   146→
   147→    # Validate skip entry kinds
   148→    for fid, entry in plan.get("skipped", {}).items():
   149→        kind = entry.get("kind")
   150→        if kind not in VALID_SKIP_KINDS:
   151→            raise ValueError(
   152→                f"Invalid skip kind {kind!r} for {fid}; must be one of {sorted(VALID_SKIP_KINDS)}"
   153→            )
   154→
   155→
   156→__all__ = [
   157→    "PLAN_VERSION",
   158→    "Cluster",
   159→    "ItemOverride",
   160→    "PlanModel",
   161→    "SkipEntry",
   162→    "SupersededEntry",
   163→    "VALID_SKIP_KINDS",
   164→    "empty_plan",
   165→    "ensure_plan_defaults",
   166→    "validate_plan",
   167→]
   168→
```

```
1→"""Post-scan plan reconciliation — handle finding churn."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from dataclasses import dataclass, field
     6→from datetime import UTC, datetime, timedelta
     7→
     8→from desloppify.engine._plan.schema import PlanModel, SupersededEntry, ensure_plan_defaults
     9→from desloppify.engine._plan.stale_dimensions import SUBJECTIVE_PREFIX
    10→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import StateModel, utc_now
    11→
    12→
    13→SUPERSEDED_TTL_DAYS = 90
    14→
    15→
    16→@dataclass
    17→class ReconcileResult:
    18→    """Summary of changes made during reconciliation."""
    19→
    20→    superseded: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    21→    pruned: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    22→    resurfaced: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
    23→    changes: int = 0
    24→
    25→
    26→def _find_candidates(
    27→    state: StateModel, detector: str, file: str
    28→) -> list[str]:
    29→    """Find open findings that could be remaps for a disappeared finding."""
    30→    candidates: list[str] = []
    31→    for fid, finding in state.get("findings", {}).items():
    32→        if finding.get("status") != "open":
    33→            continue
    34→        if finding.get("detector") == detector and finding.get("file") == file:
    35→            candidates.append(fid)
    36→    return candidates
    37→
    38→
    39→def _is_finding_alive(state: StateModel, finding_id: str) -> bool:
    40→    """Return True if the finding exists and is open."""
    41→    finding = state.get("findings", {}).get(finding_id)
    42→    if finding is None:
    43→        return False
    44→    return finding.get("status") == "open"
    45→
    46→
    47→def _supersede_id(
    48→    plan: PlanModel,
    49→    state: StateModel,
    50→    finding_id: str,
    51→    now: str,
    52→) -> bool:
    53→    """Move a disappeared finding to superseded. Returns True if changed."""
    54→    finding = state.get("findings", {}).get(finding_id)
    55→    detector = ""
    56→    file = ""
    57→    summary = ""
    58→    if finding:
    59→        detector = finding.get("detector", "")
    60→        file = finding.get("file", "")
    61→        summary = finding.get("summary", "")
    62→
    63→    candidates = _find_candidates(state, detector, file) if detector else []
    64→    # Don't include the original in candidates
    65→    candidates = [c for c in candidates if c != finding_id]
    66→
    67→    entry: SupersededEntry = {
    68→        "original_id": finding_id,
    69→        "original_detector": detector,
    70→        "original_file": file,
    71→        "original_summary": summary,
    72→        "status": "superseded",
    73→        "superseded_at": now,
    74→        "remapped_to": None,
    75→        "candidates": candidates[:5],
    76→    }
    77→
    78→    # Preserve any existing override note
    79→    override = plan.get("overrides", {}).get(finding_id)
    80→    if override and override.get("note"):
    81→        entry["note"] = override["note"]
    82→
    83→    plan["superseded"][finding_id] = entry
    84→
    85→    # Remove from queue_order, skipped, cluster finding_ids
    86→    order: list[str] = plan.get("queue_order", [])
    87→    skipped: dict = plan.get("skipped", {})
    88→    if finding_id in order:
    89→        order.remove(finding_id)
    90→    skipped.pop(finding_id, None)
    91→    for cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).values():
    92→        ids = cluster.get("finding_ids", [])
    93→        if finding_id in ids:
    94→            ids.remove(finding_id)
    95→
    96→    return True
    97→
    98→
    99→def _prune_old_superseded(plan: PlanModel, now_dt: datetime) -> list[str]:
   100→    """Remove superseded entries older than TTL. Returns pruned IDs."""
   101→    superseded = plan.get("superseded", {})
   102→    cutoff = now_dt - timedelta(days=SUPERSEDED_TTL_DAYS)
   103→    to_prune: list[str] = []
   104→
   105→    for fid, entry in superseded.items():
   106→        ts = entry.get("superseded_at", "")
   107→        try:
   108→            entry_dt = datetime.fromisoformat(ts)
   109→            if entry_dt.tzinfo is None:
   110→                entry_dt = entry_dt.replace(tzinfo=UTC)
   111→            if entry_dt < cutoff:
   112→                to_prune.append(fid)
   113→        except (ValueError, TypeError):
   114→            to_prune.append(fid)
   115→
   116→    for fid in to_prune:
   117→        superseded.pop(fid, None)
   118→        # Also clean up stale overrides
   119→        plan.get("overrides", {}).pop(fid, None)
   120→
   121→    return to_prune
   122→
   123→
   124→def reconcile_plan_after_scan(
   125→    plan: PlanModel,
   126→    state: StateModel,
   127→) -> ReconcileResult:
   128→    """Reconcile plan against current state after a scan.
   129→
   130→    Finds IDs referenced in the plan that no longer exist or are no longer
   131→    open, moves them to superseded, and prunes old superseded entries.
   132→    """
   133→    ensure_plan_defaults(plan)
   134→    result = ReconcileResult()
   135→    now = utc_now()
   136→    now_dt = datetime.now(UTC)
   137→
   138→    # Collect all finding IDs referenced by the plan
   139→    referenced_ids: set[str] = set()
   140→    referenced_ids.update(plan.get("queue_order", []))
   141→    referenced_ids.update(plan.get("skipped", {}).keys())
   142→    for override_id in plan.get("overrides", {}):
   143→        referenced_ids.add(override_id)
   144→    for cluster in plan.get("clusters", {}).values():
   145→        referenced_ids.update(cluster.get("finding_ids", []))
   146→
   147→    # Exclude already-superseded IDs and synthetic IDs (managed by stale_dimensions)
   148→    already_superseded = set(plan.get("superseded", {}).keys())
   149→    referenced_ids -= already_superseded
   150→    referenced_ids = {
   151→        fid for fid in referenced_ids
   152→        if not fid.startswith(SUBJECTIVE_PREFIX)
   153→    }
   154→
   155→    # Check each referenced ID
   156→    for fid in sorted(referenced_ids):
   157→        if not _is_finding_alive(state, fid):
   158→            if _supersede_id(plan, state, fid, now):
   159→                result.superseded.append(fid)
   160→                result.changes += 1
   161→
   162→    # Resurface stale temporary skips
   163→    scan_count = state.get("scan_count", 0)
   164→    from desloppify.engine._plan.operations import resurface_stale_skips
   165→
   166→    resurfaced = resurface_stale_skips(plan, scan_count)
   167→    if resurfaced:
   168→        result.resurfaced = resurfaced
   169→        result.changes += len(resurfaced)
   170→
   171→    # Prune old superseded entries
   172→    pruned = _prune_old_superseded(plan, now_dt)
   173→    result.pruned = pruned
   174→    result.changes += len(pruned)
   175→
   176→    return result
   177→
   178→
   179→__all__ = [
   180→    "ReconcileResult",
   181→    "reconcile_plan_after_scan",
   182→]
   183→
```

```
1→"""Ranking and grouping helpers for work queue selection."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from desloppify.engine._work_queue.helpers import (
     6→    detail_dict,
     7→    is_review_finding,
     8→    is_subjective_finding,
     9→    primary_command_for_finding,
    10→    review_finding_weight,
    11→    scope_matches,
    12→    slugify,
    13→    status_matches,
    14→    subjective_strict_scores,
    15→    supported_fixers_for_item,
    16→)
    17→from desloppify.engine.planning.common import CONFIDENCE_ORDER
    18→from desloppify.state import path_scoped_findings
    19→
    20→
    21→def subjective_score_value(item: dict) -> float:
    22→    if item.get("kind") == "subjective_dimension":
    23→        detail = detail_dict(item)
    24→        return float(detail.get("strict_score", item.get("subjective_score", 100.0)))
    25→    return float(item.get("subjective_score", 100.0))
    26→
    27→
    28→def build_finding_items(
    29→    state: dict,
    30→    *,
    31→    scan_path: str | None,
    32→    status_filter: str,
    33→    scope: str | None,
    34→    chronic: bool,
    35→) -> list[dict]:
    36→    scoped = path_scoped_findings(state.get("findings", {}), scan_path)
    37→    subjective_scores = subjective_strict_scores(state)
    38→    out: list[dict] = []
    39→
    40→    for finding_id, finding in scoped.items():
    41→        if finding.get("suppressed"):
    42→            continue
    43→        if not status_matches(finding.get("status", "open"), status_filter):
    44→            continue
    45→        if chronic and not (
    46→            finding.get("status") == "open" and finding.get("reopen_count", 0) >= 2
    47→        ):
    48→            continue
    49→
    50→        item = dict(finding)
    51→        item["id"] = finding_id
    52→        item["kind"] = "finding"
    53→        item["is_review"] = is_review_finding(item)
    54→        item["is_subjective"] = is_subjective_finding(item)
    55→        item["effective_tier"] = (
    56→            4 if item["is_subjective"] else int(finding.get("tier", 3))
    57→        )
    58→        item["review_weight"] = (
    59→            review_finding_weight(item) if item["is_review"] else None
    60→        )
    61→        subjective_score = None
    62→        if item["is_subjective"]:
    63→            detail = detail_dict(finding)
    64→            dim_name = detail.get("dimension_name", "")
    65→            dim_key = detail.get("dimension", "") or slugify(dim_name)
    66→            subjective_score = subjective_scores.get(
    67→                dim_key, subjective_scores.get(dim_name.lower(), 100.0)
    68→            )
    69→        item["subjective_score"] = subjective_score
    70→        supported_fixers = supported_fixers_for_item(state, item)
    71→        item["primary_command"] = primary_command_for_finding(
    72→            item,
    73→            supported_fixers=supported_fixers,
    74→        )
    75→
    76→        if not scope_matches(item, scope):
    77→            continue
    78→        out.append(item)
    79→
    80→    return out
    81→
    82→
    83→def item_sort_key(item: dict) -> tuple:
    84→    if item.get("kind") == "subjective_dimension" or item.get("is_subjective"):
    85→        return (
    86→            int(item.get("effective_tier", 4)),
    87→            1,  # Subjective items sort after mechanical items within T4.
    88→            subjective_score_value(item),
    89→            item.get("id", ""),
    90→        )
    91→
    92→    detail = detail_dict(item)
    93→    review_weight = float(item.get("review_weight", 0.0) or 0.0)
    94→    return (
    95→        int(item.get("effective_tier", item.get("tier", 3))),
    96→        0,
    97→        CONFIDENCE_ORDER.get(item.get("confidence", "low"), 9),
    98→        -review_weight,
    99→        -int(detail.get("count", 0) or 0),
   100→        item.get("id", ""),
   101→    )
   102→
   103→
   104→def item_explain(item: dict) -> dict:
   105→    effective_tier = int(item.get("effective_tier", item.get("tier", 3)))
   106→
   107→    if item.get("kind") == "subjective_dimension":
   108→        return {
   109→            "kind": "subjective_dimension",
   110→            "effective_tier": effective_tier,
   111→            "subjective_score": subjective_score_value(item),
   112→            "policy": (
   113→                "Subjective dimensions are always queued as T4 and do not outrank "
   114→                "mechanical T1/T2/T3 items."
   115→            ),
   116→            "ranking_factors": ["tier asc", "subjective_score asc", "id asc"],
   117→        }
   118→
   119→    detail = detail_dict(item)
   120→    confidence = item.get("confidence", "low")
   121→    is_subjective = bool(item.get("is_subjective"))
   122→    is_review = bool(item.get("is_review"))
   123→    ranking_factors: list[str]
   124→    if is_subjective:
   125→        ranking_factors = ["tier fixed to T4", "subjective_score asc", "id asc"]
   126→    elif is_review:
   127→        ranking_factors = [
   128→            "tier asc",
   129→            "confidence asc",
   130→            "review_weight desc",
   131→            "count desc",
   132→            "id asc",
   133→        ]
   134→    else:
   135→        ranking_factors = ["tier asc", "confidence asc", "count desc", "id asc"]
   136→    explain = {
   137→        "kind": "finding",
   138→        "effective_tier": effective_tier,
   139→        "confidence": confidence,
   140→        "confidence_rank": CONFIDENCE_ORDER.get(confidence, 9),
   141→        "count": int(detail.get("count", 0) or 0),
   142→        "id": item.get("id", ""),
   143→        "ranking_factors": ranking_factors,
   144→    }
   145→    if is_review:
   146→        explain["review_weight"] = float(item.get("review_weight", 0.0) or 0.0)
   147→    if is_subjective:
   148→        explain["policy"] = (
   149→            "Subjective findings are forced to T4 and do not outrank "
   150→            "mechanical T1/T2/T3 items."
   151→        )
   152→        explain["subjective_score"] = subjective_score_value(item)
   153→    return explain
   154→
   155→
   156→def tier_counts(items: list[dict]) -> dict[int, int]:
   157→    counts = {1: 0, 2: 0, 3: 0, 4: 0}
   158→    for item in items:
   159→        tier = int(item.get("effective_tier", item.get("tier", 3)))
   160→        counts[tier] = counts.get(tier, 0) + 1
   161→    return counts
   162→
   163→
   164→def choose_fallback_tier(requested_tier: int, counts: dict[int, int]) -> int | None:
   165→    available = [tier for tier, count in counts.items() if count > 0]
   166→    if not available:
   167→        return None
   168→    return min(available, key=lambda tier: (abs(tier - requested_tier), tier))
   169→
   170→
   171→def group_queue_items(items: list[dict], group: str) -> dict[str, list[dict]]:
   172→    """Group queue items for alternate output modes."""
   173→    grouped: dict[str, list[dict]] = {}
   174→    for item in items:
   175→        if group == "file":
   176→            key = item.get("file", "")
   177→        elif group == "detector":
   178→            key = item.get("detector", "")
   179→        elif group == "tier":
   180→            key = f"T{int(item.get('effective_tier', item.get('tier', 3)))}"
   181→        elif group == "cluster":
   182→            plan_cluster = item.get("plan_cluster")
   183→            key = plan_cluster["name"] if isinstance(plan_cluster, dict) else "(unclustered)"
   184→        else:
   185→            key = "items"
   186→        grouped.setdefault(key, []).append(item)
   187→    return grouped
   188→
   189→
   190→__all__ = [
   191→    "build_finding_items",
   192→    "choose_fallback_tier",
   193→    "item_explain",
   194→    "item_sort_key",
   195→    "tier_counts",
   196→    "subjective_score_value",
   197→    "group_queue_items",
   198→]
   199→
```

```
   277→def render_queue_header(queue: dict, explain: bool) -> None:
   278→    _render_tier_navigator(queue)
   279→    if not queue.get("fallback_reason"):
   280→        return
   281→    print(colorize(f"  {queue['fallback_reason']}", "yellow"))
   282→    if not explain:
   283→        return
   284→    available = queue.get("available_tiers", [])
   285→    if available:
   286→        tiers = ", ".join(f"T{tier_num}" for tier_num in available)
   287→        print(colorize(f"  explain: available tiers are {tiers}", "dim"))
   288→

   290→def show_empty_queue(queue: dict, tier: int | None, strict: float | None) -> bool:
   291→    if queue.get("items"):
   292→        return False
   293→    suffix = f" Strict score: {strict:.1f}/100" if strict is not None else ""
   294→    print(colorize(f"\n  Nothing to do!{suffix}", "green"))
   295→    if tier is not None:
   296→        print(colorize(f"  Requested tier: T{tier}", "dim"))
   297→        available = queue.get("available_tiers", [])
   298→        if available:
   299→            commands = " | ".join(
   300→                f"desloppify next --tier {tier_num}" for tier_num in available
   301→            )
   302→            print(colorize(f"  Try: {commands}", "dim"))
   303→    return True

   306→def render_terminal_items(
   307→    items: list[dict],
   308→    dim_scores: dict,
   309→    findings_scoped: dict,
   310→    *,
   311→    group: str,
   312→    explain: bool,

   316→    # Show focus header if plan has active cluster
   317→    if plan and plan.get("active_cluster"):
   318→        cluster_name = plan["active_cluster"]
   319→        clusters = plan.get("clusters", {})
   320→        cluster_data = clusters.get(cluster_name, {})
   321→        total = len(cluster_data.get("finding_ids", []))
   322→        print(colorize(f"\n  Focused on: {cluster_name} ({len(items)} of {total} remaining)", "cyan"))
   323→
   324→    if group != "item":
   325→        _render_grouped(items, group)
   326→        return
   327→    for idx, item in enumerate(items):
   328→        if idx > 0:
   329→            print()
   330→        queue_pos = item.get("queue_position")
   331→        if queue_pos and len(items) > 1:
   332→            label = f"  [#{queue_pos}]"
   333→        elif len(items) > 1:
   334→            label = f"  [{idx + 1}/{len(items)}]"
   335→        else:
   336→            pos_str = f"  (#{ queue_pos} in queue)" if queue_pos else ""
   337→            label = f"  Next item{pos_str}"
   338→        print(colorize(label, "bold"))
   339→        _render_item(item, dim_scores, findings_scoped, explain=explain, potentials=potentials)

   370→        f'    desloppify resolve wontfix "{item["id"]}" --note "<why>" '
   371→        f'--attest "{ATTEST_EXAMPLE}"'
   372→    )
   373→
   374→
   375→def render_followup_nudges(
   376→    state: dict,
   377→    dim_scores: dict,
   378→    findings_scoped: dict,
   379→    *,
   380→    strict_score: float | None,
   381→    target_strict_score: float,
   382→) -> None:
   383→    subjective_threshold = target_strict_score
   384→    subjective_entries = scorecard_subjective(state, dim_scores)
   385→    followup = build_subjective_followup(
   386→        state,
   387→        subjective_entries,
   388→        threshold=subjective_threshold,
   389→        max_quality_items=3,
   390→        max_integrity_items=5,
   391→    )
   392→    unassessed_subjective = unassessed_subjective_dimensions(dim_scores)
   393→    if strict_score is not None:
   394→        gap = round(float(target_strict_score) - float(strict_score), 1)
   395→        if gap > 0:
   396→            print(
   397→                colorize(
   398→                    f"\n  North star: strict {strict_score:.1f}/100 → target {target_strict_score:.1f} (+{gap:.1f} needed)",
   399→                    "cyan",
   400→                )
   401→            )
   402→        else:
   403→            print(
   404→                colorize(
   405→                    f"\n  North star: strict {strict_score:.1f}/100 meets target {target_strict_score:.1f}",
   406→                    "green",
   407→                )
   408→            )
   409→    # Integrity penalty/warn lines preserved (anti-gaming safeguard, must remain visible).
   410→    for style, message in followup.integrity_lines:
   411→        print(colorize(f"\n  {message}", style))
   412→
   413→    # Collapsed subjective summary.
   414→    coverage_open, _coverage_reasons, _holistic_reasons = subjective_coverage_breakdown(
   415→        findings_scoped
   416→    )
   417→    parts: list[str] = []
   418→    low_dims = len(followup.low_assessed)
   419→    unassessed_count = len(unassessed_subjective)
   420→    stale_count = sum(1 for e in subjective_entries if e.get("stale"))
   421→    open_review = [
   422→        f for f in findings_scoped.values()
   423→        if f.get("status") == "open" and f.get("detector") == "review"
   424→    ]
   425→    if low_dims:
   426→        parts.append(f"{low_dims} dimension{'s' if low_dims != 1 else ''} below target")
   427→    if stale_count:
   428→        parts.append(f"{stale_count} stale")
   429→    if unassessed_count:
   430→        parts.append(f"{unassessed_count} unassessed")
   431→    if len(open_review):
   432→        parts.append(f"{len(open_review)} review finding{'s' if len(open_review) != 1 else ''} open")
   433→    if coverage_open > 0:
   434→        parts.append(f"{coverage_open} file{'s' if coverage_open != 1 else ''} need review")
   435→
   436→    if parts:
   437→        print(colorize(f"\n  Subjective: {', '.join(parts)}.", "cyan"))
   438→        print(colorize("  Run `desloppify show subjective` for details.", "dim"))
   439→
   440→
   441→__all__ = [
   442→    "is_auto_fix_command",
   443→    "render_followup_nudges",
   444→    "render_queue_header",
   445→    "render_single_item_resolution_hint",
```
